# RRC: line-by-line marking against the golden standard

The drill itself does not change. What changes is everything after she finishes:
the recording is cut to match what was on screen, every line she rephrased is
marked, and the Report Card shows her work beside the standard.

## The drill this is grading (unchanged)

One stimulus at a time:

1. She reads only the first line, silently. Nothing is said while she is looking
   at the text.
2. She looks away and says that line out loud in her own words, from memory.
3. Back to the text, next line silently, look away, rephrase. One line at a time
   until the last line. The stimulus stays on screen throughout.
4. She presses Space once. The text disappears and she restates the whole
   stimulus out loud from memory.
5. Straight to the next stimulus. No pause, no feedback, nothing judged yet.

The whole section is one run: 20+ stimuli, one unbroken recording, one clock.

## What gets captured

- Everything spoken is transcribed with timestamps.
- Every stretch of speech is tied to the stimulus that was on screen when she
  said it, and within that stimulus to the line she had just read. The
  whole-thing recall after Space is its own stretch.
- Because she reads silently and speaks only while looking away, there is a
  silent gap before every spoken line. The line split keys off those gaps plus
  line order — it never assumes continuous talking.
- So each stimulus holds: her rephrase of each line, her full recall, and the
  audio for each.

## How the split works

No extra key presses, no change to the screen. After the run:

1. The single recording is transcribed with word-level times (already happens).
2. Within each stimulus's on-screen window, speech is broken into utterances at
   the silent gaps, and the utterances are matched in order to that stimulus's
   lines, anchored by each line's own wording.
3. A line she never spoke about is marked skipped, not credited. Two lines
   covered in one breath are attributed to both rather than losing one.
4. Speech after Space is the full recall, kept whole.

Each line ends up with its own start/end offsets into the one recording, which is
what makes per-line playback possible without splitting the file.

## How it is graded

- Nothing is graded during the run. Grading runs once, the moment she submits the
  finished section, and covers every stimulus in that pass.
- Each stimulus is compared against the golden standard already stored for it:
  the standard recording's transcript plus the authored key points.
- The bar is **same points, same quality — not same words**. Phrasing may differ.
  Full marks means every point the standard makes is carried at the same level.
  Saying a line back word for word is not a rephrase and does not count.
- For each stimulus: line by line, what she carried and what she dropped; then
  the same for the full recall; then the big misses named.
- Then one overall verdict for the section: band, coverage, time, pattern note.

## What she sees on the Report Card

Per stimulus, per line: the line as written, its key points, what she said, what
she carried and dropped, and a play button for exactly that stretch of her
recording. Then the full recall marked the same way, with "hear it done right"
from the stored standard. Then the section verdict at the top.

## Answer key check

Before building, an audit of every section in the library: every stimulus has
lines, every line has key points, and a standard recording plus transcript exists
for it. Anything missing is listed for you rather than silently graded as zero.

## Technical notes

- `useSectionRecorder` / `SectionRunner` unchanged; they keep stamping the
  existing `working` / `recalling` windows per stimulus.
- New alignment step in `grade-rrc-section`: cut the Deepgram word stream by
  `rrc.audio_segments`, split the `working` words into utterances on inter-word
  gaps (threshold tuned on real audio, defaulting around 1.2s), then attribute
  utteran...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# 0015 — The Model Run is reference data, not content

Date: 2026-09-01
Status: accepted
Extends [0010](./0010-rrc-section-is-the-unit-of-work.md).

## Context

Every section item carried two strings: `model_recording: "audio/PT105_S2_Q1.mp3"`
and `model_transcript: "transcripts/PT105_S2_Q1.txt"`. Both are rootless. They
resolve against one folder on one Mac and against nothing that deploys. Nothing
in the app read either, so this was latent rather than broken, but the grader
that is coming is server-side and cannot read that disk.

The transcripts are the priority for truth. They are the record of the drill done
correctly and the standard the 1,294 authored Details were calibrated against, so
grading depends on them in a way it does not depend on the audio.

The obvious fix is to inline the transcript text beside `the_read`, which is
already carried as text. One verified fact rules it out. `getStudentAssignment`
selects `*` from `activity_assignments`, whose student policy is
`student_id = auth.uid()`, and Postgres row-level security filters rows rather
than columns. Everything in `items` reaches her browser. That is already true of
the Details, of `the_read`, and of `correct_answer`, which is a separate problem
this ADR does not fix; it must not also become true of his verbatim recall of
every stimulus in the section she is about to run.

## Decision

**The Model Run is reference data with its own home.** `public.model_runs`, one
row per captured stimulus, holding the transcript as a column and the recording
as a `(bucket, path, sha256)` pointer into a private `model-runs` bucket.

**An item joins to it on the slug it already has.** `PT105_S2_Q1` is the
capture's own identifier and is already the item's `slug`, so there is no new
key and nothing to keep in step. Both path fields come out of `items` entirely.

**Their absence is enforced by the fixture test**, not left as a convention. The
build still refuses to assemble a section unless both halves exist on disk; it
just no longer ships their names.

**Writes are admin, not service-role.** There is no service-role key outside
Lovable, so a service-role-only table could be loaded only by pasting 621
transcripts into a browser SQL editor. Admin insert/update policies let
`load_model_runs.py` load it over PostgREST with the admin JWT already in
`~/ai-os/.env`, and keep 2.6MB of his spoken keys out of this repository's git
history, which a seed migration would not.

**Versioned, because re-transcription is coming.** 93 of the 714 recordings are
still untranscribed and the rest were produced by one Whisper pass. A better pass
writes `version = 2` and archives the old row rather than overwriting the text a
section's Details were calibrated against. A partial unique index keeps exactly
one active row per slug.

## Consequences

**The transcript is unreachable by a student by construction.** `model_runs` has
no policy granting `authenticated` select. That absence is the security property,
and it is durable in a way a column exclusion is not.

**The 1.1GB upload is no longer blocked on a credential.** It was recorded as
blocked on a service role key. With admin write policies it needs the two
migrations applied and nothing else.

**Two migrations have to be applied before anything loads.** They ship in the
repo; applying them is a step in Lovable, not a push.

**A future playback gate must not trust `activity_assignments.status`.** Students
hold UPDATE on their own assignment row, so the obvious rule for unlocking his
recording after she finishes, "her status is completed", is a boolean she can
set herself. Whatever unlocks playback has to be derived from work she actually
submitted.

**The seed shrinks slightly** and the three sections were rebuilt and re-seeded.

## Alternatives considered

**Inline `model_transcript_text` in `items`.** Rejected on the leak above. It
would also duplicate into every per...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

[external_agent_tool_result]
-- Where Joshua's own run of a stimulus lives.
--
-- Read, Recall, Colour is graded against two different keys and they have
-- opposite audiences. The Details are the key her recall is scored against and
-- they travel with the Activity. His Model Run -- the recording of the drill
-- done correctly, and the transcript of it -- is the standard those Details
-- were calibrated against, and she must never read it before she has run the
-- section. Until now both were a rootless string in the content JSON
-- ("audio/PT105_S2_Q1.mp3") that resolved only against a folder on his Mac, so
-- nothing deployed could reach either one.
--
-- This is the home. One row per captured stimulus, keyed by the slug the
-- content already carries, so an Activity item joins to its Model Run on the
-- identifier it has rather than on a path it has to be told.
--
-- Why the transcript is a column and the audio is a pointer: the transcript is
-- 2KB of text a grader reads server-side and never streams, so a query returns
-- it in one round trip. The recording is 1.6MB she plays back in her review,
-- and 714 of them are 1.1GB, so it belongs in a bucket with a signed URL.
--
-- Why it is not in the Activity's `items`: a student fetching her own
-- assignment selects every column of it (activitiesClient.getStudentAssignment),
-- and Postgres RLS filters rows, never columns. Anything in `items` is in her
-- browser. That is already true of the Details and of The Read's key, which is
-- its own problem; it must not become true of his verbatim run of the drill.

create table if not exists public.model_runs (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),

  -- The join key, and the same string the content item carries as its slug.
  slug text not null,
  -- Re-transcribing the corpus with a better model writes a new version rather
  -- than overwriting the text a section's Details were calibrated against.
  version integer not null default 1,
  status text not null default 'active'
    check (status in ('active', 'archived')),

  source_section text not null,        -- PT105.S2
  citation text,                       -- PT105.S2.Q1

  -- The recording. Bucket and path rather than a URL: a signed URL expires, and
  -- the capture's own Airtable URLs already carry an expiry stamp.
  audio_bucket text not null default 'model-runs',
  audio_path text not null,
  audio_bytes bigint,
  audio_sha256 char(64),

  -- The transcript. Nullable on purpose and not a defect: 93 of the 714
  -- recordings are not transcribed yet, and a row that carries the audio with
  -- no transcript is the honest record of that.
  transcript_text text,
  transcript_sha256 char(64),
  asr_engine text,
  asr_model text,
  transcribed_at timestamptz,

  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  updated_at timestamptz not null default now(),

  unique (slug, version),
  -- The hash is what a drift check compares, so text without one is a row that
  -- cannot be checked and must not exist.
  constraint model_runs_transcript_pairing
    check ((transcript_text is null) = (transcript_sha256 is null))
);

-- One row per slug is the current truth; the rest are history.
create unique index if not exists model_runs_one_active_per_slug
  on public.model_runs (slug) where status = 'active';
create index if not exists model_runs_section_idx
  on public.model_runs (source_section, status);

drop trigger if exists model_runs_updated_at on public.model_runs;
create trigger model_runs_updated_at
  before update on public.model_runs
  for each row execute function public.update_updated_at_column();

alter table public.model_runs enable row level security;

-- One policy per command rather than one FOR ALL, so widening a write later
-- cannot silently widen the read. There is deliberately NO policy granting a
-- student select: that absence is the whole security property of this table.
drop policy if exists "admi...

import fs from "node:fs"; import path from "node:path";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
function readEnv(f: string){const o:Record<string,string>={};if(!fs.existsSync(f))return o;for(const l of fs.readFileSync(f,"utf8").split(/\r?\n/)){const t=l.trim();if(!t||t.startsWith("#"))continue;const i=t.indexOf("=");if(i<0)continue;o[t.slice(0,i).trim()]=t.slice(i+1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g,"");}return o;}
const env={...readEnv(path.resolve("../../.env")),...readEnv(".env")};
const {error}=await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({email:env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,password:[REDACTED]}); if(error) throw error;
const {count:total}=await supabase.from("model_runs").select("*",{count:"exact",head:true});
const {count:active}=await supabase.from("model_runs").select("*",{count:"exact",head:true}).eq("status","active");
const {count:withTx}=await supabase.from("model_runs").select("*",{count:"exact",head:true}).eq("status","active").not("transcript_text","is",null);
console.log("model_runs rows:",total,"| active:",active,"| active WITH transcript:",withTx,"| active MISSING transcript:",(active??0)-(withTx??0));
const rows:any[]=[]; for(let f=0;f<2000;f+=1000){const {data}=await supabase.from("model_runs").select("slug,source_section,audio_path,audio_bytes,transcript_text,asr_model").eq("status","active").range(f,f+999); if(!data?.length)break; rows.push(...data);}
const bySec=new Map<string,{n:number;tx:number;audio:number}>();
for(const r of rows){const s=bySec.get(r.source_section)??{n:0,tx:0,audio:0};s.n++;if(r.transcript_text)s.tx++;if(r.audio_path)s.audio++;bySec.set(r.source_section,s);}
console.log("distinct sections:",bySec.size);
console.log("rows with audio_path:",rows.filter(r=>r.audio_path).length,"| audio_bytes present:",rows.filter(r=>r.audio_bytes).length);
console.log("asr models:",[...new Set(rows.map(r=>r.asr_model))].join(", "));
for(const s of ["PT105.S2","PT102.S3","PT134.S3"]){const v=bySec.get(s);console.log("  LIVE SECTION",s,v?`${v.n} stimuli, ${v.tx} transcribed, ${v.audio} with audio`:"NONE");}
const samp=rows.find(r=>r.source_section==="PT105.S2"&&r.slug.endsWith("_Q1"));
console.log("\nsample",samp?.slug,"audio:",samp?.audio_path,samp?.audio_bytes,"bytes\ntranscript head:",(samp?.transcript_text??"").slice(0,400));
EOF

/**
 * Student: the drill done properly, for a section she has already been graded
 * on.
 *
 * `model_runs` holds 714 recordings across 109 sections, one per stimulus, and
 * until now every one of them was admin-only. RLS is what makes this safe to
 * ask for at all: the policy returns rows only for a section she has a Report
 * Card for, so the recording becomes reachable at the moment it stops being
 * able to help her and starts being able to teach her.
 *
 * Keyed by stimulus slug, because that is what the grade carries. An empty map
 * is the ordinary answer for a section nobody has finished, and the Report Card
 * simply does not offer the button.
 */
export type ModelRun = {
  /** The run as a readable document. Present for every stimulus in a built section. */
  walkthrough: string | null;
  /** The recording, when there is one. Nine of the built stimuli were never recorded. */
  audio: { bucket: string; path: string } | null;
};

/**
 * The model runs for a section, keyed by stimulus slug.
 *
 * Written-first. This used to return `{bucket, path}` and drop any row without
 * both, which encoded "a model run is a recording" one layer above the schema
 * that said the same thing. It meant the nine authored references were silently
 * invisible, and that a student on a slow connection got nothing rather than the
 * text of the same run.
 *
 * A row survives here if it can offer her either one.
 */
export async function listModelRuns(
  sourceSection: string,
): Promise<Record<string, ModelRun>> {
  const table = supabase.from("model_runs" as never) as unknown as {
    select: (columns: string) => {
      eq: (
        column: "source_section",
        value: string,
      ) => Promise<{ data: unknown; error: { code?: string; message?: string } | null }>;
    };
  };
  const { data, error } = await table
    .select("slug, audio_bucket, audio_path, walkthrough_md")
    .eq("source_section", sourceSection);
  if (error) {
    // A table that is not there yet, or a policy that says no. Neither is worth
    // failing a Report Card over. `walkthrough_md` is covered by the same
    // unknown-column branch, so a deploy that runs ahead of the migration
    // degrades to no model runs rather than to a broken card.
    if (isUnknownColumn(error) || error.code === "42P01") return {};
    console.error("[rrc] could not load the model runs", error);
    return {};
  }
  const rows =
    (data as
      | {
          slug: string;
          audio_bucket: string | null;
          audio_path: string | null;
          walkthrough_md: string | null;
        }[]
      | null) ?? [];
  return Object.fromEntries(
    rows
      .map((r) => {
        const audio =
          r.audio_bucket && r.audio_path ? { bucket: r.audio_bucket, path: r.audio_path } : null;
        const walkthrough = r.walkthrough_md?.trim() ? r.walkthrough_md : null;
        return [r.slug, { walkthrough, audio }] as const;
      })
      .filter(([, run]) => run.walkthrough || run.audio),
  );
}

/**
 * One run as this read returns it.
 *
 * `SectionGradeStub` is the light shape, and this read is deliberately heavier:
 * it carries `stimuli` so a caller can say something per stimulus rather than
 * per run, and `created_at` because `graded_at` is not stable.
 *
 * A regrade rewrites `graded_at`. Order or date a list by it and a regraded run
 * from a fortnight ago climbs above this morning's, becomes "your last run", and
 * takes the trend with it. `created_at` is written once and never touched.
 */
export type RecentSectionRun = SectionGradeStub &

Yes. It's real and it's live. I verified it against the production database, not just the code.

**What exists right now**

- **714 MP3s of you doing the drill** sit in the private `model-runs` Supabase bucket. I listed the bucket: 714 objects. I cross-checked every database row's `audio_path` against it — **0 missing files**.
- **723 rows** in `model_runs`, covering **109 sections**, **713 with a Deepgram nova-3 transcript**.
- I minted a signed URL for `PT105_S2_Q1.mp3` and actually fetched it: **HTTP 206, `audio/mpeg`, bytes returned**. The file plays.

**Coverage on the three live sections is complete — every question:**

| Section | Stimuli | Audio in bucket | Written walkthrough |
|---|---|---|---|
| PT105.S2 | 25 | 25 | 23 |
| PT102.S3 | 24 | 24 | 22 |
| PT134.S3 | 26 | 26 | 26 |

**Can a student press play?** Yes. On the Report Card, each question shows a transport with two buttons side by side: **"Hear it back"** (her recording) and **"Hear it done right"** (yours). `SectionReportCard.tsx:839`.

The gate is an RLS policy, not a flag: she can reach the recording for a section **only after she has a grade for it**. Before that the query returns an empty map and the button simply isn't rendered.

**Three honest caveats**

1. **It's per question, not per line.** "Hear it done right" plays your whole run of that stimulus. The code says why outright: the grader stores start/end timings for every line *she* spoke and nothing equivalent for your recording, so highlighting through yours line by line would mean inventing the timings. Line-by-line pairing exists only for the *written* walkthrough, and only when your run has exactly one recall per authored line.
2. **Written walkthroughs lag the audio.** 283 of 723 rows have `walkthrough_md`. On the live sections it's nearly complete (23/25, 22/24, 26/26), but corpus-wide most rows are audio-only.
3. **I verified as admin, not as a student.** The student-facing policy ships in `20260903210000_she_hears_it_done_properly.sql`. Strong evidence it's applied: a later migration's column (`walkthrough_md`, from 20260904120000) returned data from the live DB, so migrations past that point are in. But I did not log in as a student and click the button. If you want certainty on the gate itself, that's the one test left, and I can run it.

So the short version: the golden standards aren't a sample or a plan. All 75 questions across the three live sections have your recording, and the play button is wired.

---
name: firecrawl-deep-research
description: |
  Produce an intensive, cited analytical report: executive summary, multi-angle
  findings, contrarian views, open questions, and full sources. Use only when the
  user needs rigorous synthesis of a complex topic (scientific, technical, policy,
  or market-analytical) that cannot be answered with a short search, and wants
  a formal written report, not a recommendation list.

  Do not use for product picks, top-N lists, quick lookups, or routine "find out
  about X" tasks. If the request does not clearly need this kind of report, do
  not use this skill.

  Do not use for a literature review over published papers. This skill collects
  evidence from the open web. A request for the literature on a biomedical,
  clinical, life-science, or other scientific topic — papers, studies, trials,
  preprints — belongs to firecrawl-research-papers, which queries Firecrawl's
  paper index (PubMed, bioRxiv, medRxiv, arXiv) instead of searching websites.
license: ISC
metadata:
  author: firecrawl
  version: "0.1.0"
  homepage: https://www.firecrawl.dev
  source: https://github.com/firecrawl/firecrawl-workflows
inputs:
  - name: FIRECRAWL_API_KEY
    description: Firecrawl API key for hosted Firecrawl requests.
    required: true
---

# Firecrawl Deep Research

Use this only for report-scale research: a rigorous, cited synthesis the user
explicitly wants delivered as a formal written report. If the request is a
product pick, a top-N list, a quick lookup, or anything answerable with a short
search, stop; do not use this skill, let the request be handled the standard
way.

This skill gathers its evidence from the open web. If the evidence base is the
published literature — a literature review, or a biomedical, clinical, or other
scientific topic where the answer lives in papers — use
[firecrawl-research-papers](../firecrawl-research-papers/SKILL.md) instead; it
queries Firecrawl's paper index rather than searching websites.

## Onboarding Interview

Infer the topic and output format from context. Before starting, unless already specified, always ask one short question to define the scope:

> "How long do you want this research task to run?"

Map the answer to a depth tier in the Collection Plan below:
- A few minutes → Quick
- ~10-15 minutes → Thorough
- Longer / no limit → Exhaustive

If the topic itself is unclear, you may ask at most 1-2 additional concise
questions (topic, or a critical angle/source constraint). Otherwise proceed once
the runtime is set.

## Firecrawl Collection Plan
[/external_agent_tool_result]

  -h, --help                                   display help for command

Commands:
  scrape [options] [urls...]                   Scrape one or more URLs. Multiple URLs are scraped concurrently and saved to .firecrawl/
  crawl [options] [url-or-job-id]              Crawl a website using Firecrawl
  map [options] [url]                          Map URLs on a website using Firecrawl
  parse [options] <file>                       Parse a local file (HTML, PDF, DOCX, DOC, ODT, RTF, XLSX, XLS) into markdown, HTML, links, JSON, and more. Uses /v2/parse.
  monitor                                      Schedule recurring scrapes/crawls/searches and track content changes
  search [options] <query>                     Search the web using Firecrawl
  developer [options] <query>                  Search an index built for coding agents: GitHub issues, merged PRs, repository READMEs, and curated documentation sites. Express repository, source, language, topic, license, and other scoping intent in the query text; semantic retrieval handles the scoping.
  research                                     Search Firecrawl's research paper index: ~43M abstracts, around 90% biomedical (PubMed, bioRxiv, medRxiv) plus arXiv. Use this for biomedical, clinical, and scientific literature instead of scraping PubMed, bioRxiv, or Google Scholar by hand. Also searches GitHub issue/PR history.
  feedback [options] <endpoint> <jobId>        Send feedback on a Firecrawl endpoint job.
  search-feedback [options] <searchId>         Send feedback on a previous search result. Refunds 1 credit on first submission.
  agent [options] <prompt-or-job-id>           Run an AI agent to extract data from the web
  interact [options] [args...]                 Interact with a scraped page in a live browser session. Run AI prompts or execute code against any previous scrape.
  experimental|x                               Experimental commands (download)
  config [options]                             Configure Firecrawl (login if not authenticated)
  view-config                                  View current configuration and authentication status
  login [options]                              Login to Firecrawl (alias for config)
  logout                                       Logout and clear stored credentials
  init [options] [template]                    Set up Firecrawl: install CLI, authenticate, add integrations, and scaffold a template
  setup [options] [subcommand]                 Set up individual firecrawl integrations (core, build, workflows, mcp, defaults)
  make [options] <target>                      Make Firecrawl the default provider for supported workflows
  launch|launcher [options] [agent] [args...]  Configure Firecrawl MCP for an AI agent, then launch it
  env [options]                                Pull FIRECRAWL_API_KEY into a local .env file
  credit-usage [options]                       Get team credit usage information
  doctor [options] [job-id]                    Run environment diagnostics, or diagnose a specific run by job ID
  version [options]                            Display version information
  help [command]                               display help for command
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Write research report to vault
command: cat > ~/dev/ai-os/wiki/VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md <<'MDEOF'
# Making videos from Claude Code: the options, priced

Date: 2026-09-09. Method: five Firecrawl `agent` jobs (spark-1-pro) plus two
developer-index searches. All figures are from official pricing/licence pages
retrieved on this date and are cited below. Related: [[tasks]], [[hot]].

## The recommendation

**Remotion, driven by Claude Code through Remotion's own agent skills.** For a
solo operator it is free, including commercial use and automated local
rendering, so it costs nothing beyond the Claude Code membership. That was the
constraint, and it is the only serious option that meets it.

Install path:

```bash
npx create-video@latest
npx remotion skills add
```

There is also an official Claude Code plugin: `remotion-dev/claude-code-plugin`.
The older official Remotion MCP is **deprecated** — the docs say not to use it
for new installs. Skills/plugin is the current path.

## Why Remotion and not the alternatives

| Option | Licence | Cost for a solo operator | LLM fit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Remotion** 4.0.523 | Source-available, proprietary | **Free** (orgs ≤3 people, all features, unlimited commercial use) | Excellent — official Claude Code skills + plugin |
| Revideo 0.11.0 | MIT | Free | Good, plain TypeScript, but no narration layer |
| Manim / ManimGL | MIT | Free | Good for math/diagram animation only |

The Remotion licence has a trap worth knowing even though it does not bite you:
organizations of **4 or more people** need a Company License, and the
"Automators" tier ($0.01/render, **$100/month minimum**) covers batch rendering,
prompt-to-video apps, and automated video products. At your size the Free
License covers all of it. If Aspiring Attorneys ever passes three people, that
changes — it is a headcount trigger, not a usage trigger. Verify the terms
yourself before relying on it commercially; this is a licence, not a spec.

## Working pipelines other people have built

- **`runesleo/claude-video-kit`** (MIT, 119 stars) — closest to your use case. A
  JSON script becomes a 1080x1920 explainer MP4 via one `render.sh`: TTS,
  Whisper-aligned captions, Remotion slides.
- **`Bhonar/docs-to-video`** — MCP extracts a page, a Claude Code skill writes the
  scenes, ElevenLabs narrates with real word timestamps, Remotion renders.
  Strongest repository evidence of the four.
- **`Agents365-ai/video-podcast-maker`** (MIT, 1.6k stars) — script-driven 4K
  narrated video, local Edge/Azure TTS, CI smoke-render.
- **`itsjwill/vanta`** (MIT) — voice cloning + WhisperX word-accurate captions.
- **`Yusuke710/manim-skill`** — Claude Code skill: plan, write Manim, render,
  watch it, iterate.

## The narration question

You do not need a TTS vendor. `model_runs` already holds **714 recordings of you
doing the drill**, 713 transcribed with word timings. For RRC videos the
narration is already recorded, in your own voice, and Remotion can lay
transcript-timed captions against it.

You also already have an ElevenLabs-family MCP connected in this session
(`generate_audio`, `create_voice`, `generate_video`, `shorts_studio`) if you want
synthetic narration for scripts you have not recorded.

## If you ever want generated footage instead of rendered graphics

Cheapest per finished minute, API rates as of 2026-09-09:

| Model | Rate | Per finished minute | Audio | Max clip |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Veo 3.1 Lite** 720p | $0.05/sec | **$3.00** | Yes | 8s |
| Veo 3.1 Fast 720p | $0.10/sec | $6.00 | Yes | 8s |
| Sora 2 Standard 720p | $0.10/sec | $6.00 | Yes | 20s |
| Veo 3.1 Standard | $0.40/sec | $24.00 | Yes | 8s |

**Sora 2's API is deprecated and scheduled to shut down 2026-09-24.** Do not
build on it.

Avatar/talking-head, if you ever want a synthetic you on camera:

| Vendor | Price/min | Custom avatar of yourself |
|---|---|---|
| HeyGen Avatar III | $1.00 | Yes on web plans; **API digital twin is Enterprise only** |
| Synthesia Starter | ~$1.90 | Yes, 3 personal avatars |
| Argil Classic | ~$4.16 | Yes |
| Captions.ai Mirage | $9.00 | Yes |

For teaching content none of this beats screen-rendered graphics plus your real
voice. Generated footage is for atmosphere, not instruction.

Done. Report saved to [VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md](wiki/VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md).

**First, the thing you should know:** the research cost **4 Firecrawl credits**. You went from 10,597 to 10,593. Five `spark-1-pro` agents and two developer-index searches barely registered. There's no way to meaningfully drain that balance on a research task — agent jobs are priced per job, not per page crawled. If burning the credits is a goal in itself, it'd take bulk crawling at a scale that produces data you don't need. I'd let them sit.

**The answer: Remotion, driven by Claude Code through Remotion's own agent skills.**

```bash
npx create-video@latest
npx remotion skills add
```

It meets your actual constraint exactly. Remotion's Free License covers **individuals and organizations up to 3 people** — all features, unlimited commercial use, local and automated rendering included. You render on your own machine. Cost beyond your Claude Code membership: **zero**.

Two things worth flagging:

- The Company License trigger is **headcount, not usage**. At 4+ people the "Automators" tier kicks in at $0.01/render with a **$100/month minimum**. You're nowhere near it, but it's a cliff, not a slope. It's a licence — verify the terms yourself before you build a business process on my summary.
- The **official Remotion MCP is deprecated**. Docs explicitly say don't use it for new installs. Skills and the `remotion-dev/claude-code-plugin` are the live path. Anyone telling you to install the MCP is working from stale info.

**The best existing repo for your use case** is `runesleo/claude-video-kit` (MIT) — a JSON script becomes a finished explainer MP4 through one `render.sh`: TTS, Whisper-aligned captions, Remotion slides. `Bhonar/docs-to-video` has the strongest evidence of actually working end to end.

**The connection you may not have made:** you don't need a TTS vendor for RRC videos. `model_runs` already holds 714 recordings of you doing the drill, 713 transcribed with word timings. The narration is recorded, in your voice, and Remotion can lay transcript-timed captions against it. You also have an ElevenLabs-family MCP already connected in this session if you want synthetic narration for unrecorded scripts.

Generated footage (Veo 3.1 Lite is cheapest at $3.00/finished minute) and avatars (HeyGen $1/min, but API digital twins are Enterprise-only) are in the report. For teaching content neither beats rendered graphics plus your real voice.

One correction on Sora: **its API is deprecated and shuts down 2026-09-24.** Don't build on it.

## Start the preview [​](https://www.remotion.dev/docs/ai/coding-agents\#start-the-preview "Direct link to Start the preview")
First, go into the directory that was created.
If you named your project `my-video`, you would run the following command:
```
cd my-videoCopy
```
Install dependencies
```
npm installCopy
```
Install the [Remotion Agent Skills](https://www.remotion.dev/docs/ai/skills):
```
Terminal
npx remotion skills addCopy
```
Start the project using the following command:
```
npm run devCopy
```
## Start your coding agent [​](https://www.remotion.dev/docs/ai/coding-agents\#start-your-coding-agent "Direct link to Start your coding agent")
Open a separate terminal window and start your coding agent of choice:
```
cd my-video
claude # or: codex / kimi / opencodeCopy
```
You can now prompt a video! See our video above for a few ideas on how to start.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Save rspack gotcha to memory
command: cat > ~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/remotion-rspack-emits-no-js.md <<'EOF'
---
name: remotion-rspack-emits-no-js
description: Remotion's rspack bundler silently emits zero JS on this machine; the ENOENT bundle.js error is a decoy.
metadata:
  type: project
---

In a Remotion project on this Windows box, `Config.setRspack(true)` (which
`create-video` writes by default when you accept Tailwind) makes `remotion
bundle` print "Bundling 100%", exit 0, and emit **no JavaScript** — `build/`
contains only `index.html` and `favicon.ico`.

Every later command then fails with `ENOENT: no such file or directory, open
'...bundle.js'`. That error is a decoy: it is Remotion's source-map reader
failing to symbolicate the real error, not the real error. Chasing it leads
nowhere.

**Fix:** comment out `Config.setRspack(true)` in `remotion.config.ts` and let it
use webpack. Verified 2026-09-09 by rendering MyComp to a real 60-frame MP4.

Second gotcha in the same setup: npm here blocks install scripts, so
`esbuild`'s postinstall never runs. Fix with `npm approve-scripts esbuild`.

Project lives at `dev/ai-os/projects/video`. See
[[video-generation-stack]] and [[lsatu-content-lives-in-postgres]].
EOF
printf '%s\n' '- [Remotion rspack emits no JS](remotion-rspack-emits-no-js.md) — the ENOENT bundle.js error is a decoy; turn rspack off.' >> ~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/MEMORY.md
echo saved
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

Set up and verified working.

**What Firecrawl turned up:** the plugin repo itself is a dead end — `remotion-dev/claude-code-plugin`'s README says only *"This is an internal package and has no documentation."* The real install commands were on the docs page, which was last updated **today**:

```bash
claude plugin marketplace add remotion-dev/claude-code-plugin
claude plugin install remotion@remotion
```

Both came back **already installed** (user scope) — the scaffold's "Add agent skills?" step had done it. So the plugin was in place before I ran a thing.

**What's set up** at [projects/video](projects/video):

- Blank Remotion project, 4.0.523, Tailwind v4, 368 packages
- Remotion agent skills pinned in `skills-lock.json`: `remotion-best-practices`, `remotion-captions`, `remotion-create`, `remotion-docs`, `remotion-interactivity`, and more
- `remotion@remotion` plugin active

**Two real bugs, both fixed.** It did not work out of the box:

1. **npm blocked `esbuild`'s postinstall**, so its binary never installed. Fixed with `npm approve-scripts esbuild`.
2. **rspack silently emits zero JavaScript.** `create-video` writes `Config.setRspack(true)` when you accept Tailwind. It prints "Bundling 100%", exits 0, and produces only `index.html` and `favicon.ico` — no JS. Every later command then dies with `ENOENT ... bundle.js`, which is a decoy: that's Remotion's source-map reader failing, not the actual fault. I chased it twice before spotting it. Switched to webpack.

I left a comment block in `remotion.config.ts` explaining why rspack is off, so it doesn't get switched back, and saved it to memory.

**Verified, not assumed:** `MyComp` rendered 60 frames to a real 5.4 KB MP4 at 1280x720. The file's attached.

To start working:

```bash
cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && npm run dev
```

Then open Claude Code in that folder and prompt for a video — the skills load automatically.

One caveat: I rendered from a pre-built bundle (`npx remotion render build MyComp out.mp4`). The temp-directory path that `remotion render` uses by default also hit an ENOENT, which may be the same rspack cache or something in your temp folder. Bundling to `build/` first sidesteps it. I haven't isolated that one — say the word and I'll chase it down.

import { useEffect, useRef, type HTMLAttributes } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase, SectionStimulus } from "./section";
import type { CoachNote } from "./coach";
import { VoiceLevel } from "./VoiceLevel";
import { useNoKeyboard } from "./noKeyboard";

/**
 * Is this page running inside another page's frame?
 *
 * A preview frame is never handed the microphone: the embed carries no
 * microphone permission, so `getUserMedia` is refused before the student is
 * ever asked. That looks identical to a student saying no, and "allow the
 * microphone for this site" is advice she cannot act on. So when the drill is
 * framed, the refusal names the real cause and offers the one thing that
 * works: the same address in a tab of its own.
 *
 * A cross-origin frame throws on the read, and a throw means framed.
 */
function isEmbedded(): boolean {
  try {
    return window.self !== window.top;
  } catch {
    return true;
  }
}


/**
 * One screen of a Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run.
 *
 * Presentational, like `RecallStage`, and separate from it because the rule it
 * keeps is different. v1 hides one chunk during its recall; a section has two
 * looks and nothing between them: the whole stimulus while she works it, and
 * no stimulus at all during The Recall
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
 *
 * **It wears the real section's clothes.** The drill she is training for is sat
 * on LawHub, and `OfficialDrillFrame` already mirrors that surface: indigo
 * field, white header rail, one centred white paper card, the stimulus in
 * Fraunces at 19px. This screen replicates that contract exactly and removes
 * the answering half -- no question column, no choices, no verdict, no
 * notepad -- so the drill feels like the thing it is preparing her for rather
 * than like a separate app. The frame itself is not imported because it carries
 * auth, the notepad and the tutor toggle, none of which may exist inside this
 * loop, and because this component has to stay presentational.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

Capture is at [brainstorms/2026-09-09-rrc-instructional-video.md](brainstorms/2026-09-09-rrc-instructional-video.md). I'll checkpoint after every answer.

**The thing I need to settle first.** You said the video should show the question "in the same way they would see it." But your description of the drill — read one line, look away, say it, next line — isn't what the app renders. In `working` phase the **entire stimulus is on screen the whole time**, every chunk shown at once with nothing marking one out from another. The app deliberately doesn't track which line she's on (ADR 0013). Space is pressed **twice** per stimulus, not once.

So the drill is a discipline she imposes on a static screen, not something the screen walks her through. A video showing lines appearing one at a time would teach the wrong thing — but a video of text just sitting there while you talk for 90 seconds teaches nothing either. That tension is the whole design problem.

### Q1 - Screen fidelity vs teaching the invisible discipline
- Asked: The app shows the whole stimulus at once and never highlights a line.
  How should the video show what she is supposed to be doing internally?
- Captured: **Exact screen + a teaching overlay.** Render the real runner screen
  pixel-accurate (whole stimulus visible, nothing removed), and add a clearly
  distinct overlay layer: a soft highlight on the chunk currently being worked,
  plus a side caption naming the step.
- Decision consequence: the overlay must be *visually unmistakable as
  instruction*, not as UI, or she will expect a highlight in the real drill.
  Treat that as a hard design constraint on the overlay styling.
- Flags: none
EOF

import fs from "node:fs"; import path from "node:path";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
function readEnv(f: string){const o:Record<string,string>={};if(!fs.existsSync(f))return o;for(const l of fs.readFileSync(f,"utf8").split(/\r?\n/)){const t=l.trim();if(!t||t.startsWith("#"))continue;const i=t.indexOf("=");if(i<0)continue;o[t.slice(0,i).trim()]=t.slice(i+1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g,"");}return o;}
const env={...readEnv(path.resolve("../../.env")),...readEnv(".env")};
const {error}=await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({email:env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,password:[REDACTED]}); if(error) throw error;
const OUT=path.resolve("../video/public/audio");
const slugs=["PT105_S2_Q1","PT105_S2_Q2","PT105_S2_Q3"];
const meta:any={};
for(const slug of slugs){
  const {data,error:e}=await supabase.from("model_runs").select("slug,audio_path,audio_bytes,transcript_text,source_section,citation").eq("slug",slug).eq("status","active").single();
  if(e) throw new Error(slug+": "+e.message);
  const {data:blob,error:de}=await supabase.storage.from("model-runs").download(data.audio_path);
  if(de) throw new Error("download "+slug+": "+de.message);
  const buf=Buffer.from(await blob.arrayBuffer());
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(OUT,slug+".mp3"),buf);
  meta[slug]={transcript:data.transcript_text,bytes:buf.length,audio_path:data.audio_path,citation:data.citation};
  console.log(slug,"->",buf.length,"bytes  (db said",data.audio_bytes+")", buf.length===data.audio_bytes?"MATCH":"*** MISMATCH ***");
}
fs.writeFileSync(path.resolve("../video/public/audio/transcripts.json"),JSON.stringify(meta,null,2));
console.log("transcripts.json written");
EOF
npx vite-node scripts/_dl_rrc_tmp.ts 2>&1 | tail -8

import json, sys, pathlib
from faster_whisper import WhisperModel

AUDIO = pathlib.Path("public/audio")
OUT = pathlib.Path("align")
model_size = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "small.en"

print(f"loading {model_size} ...", flush=True)
model = WhisperModel(model_size, device="cpu", compute_type="int8")

for slug in ["PT105_S2_Q1", "PT105_S2_Q2", "PT105_S2_Q3"]:
    path = AUDIO / f"{slug}.mp3"
    segments, info = model.transcribe(
        str(path), word_timestamps=True, vad_filter=False, beam_size=5, language="en"
    )
    words = []
    for seg in segments:
        for w in (seg.words or []):
            words.append({"w": w.word.strip(), "start": round(w.start, 3), "end": round(w.end, 3)})
    (OUT / f"{slug}.words.json").write_text(json.dumps(words, indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
    text = " ".join(x["w"] for x in words)
    print(f"{slug}: {len(words)} words, audio {info.duration:.1f}s, last word ends {words[-1]['end']:.1f}s", flush=True)
    (OUT / f"{slug}.whisper.txt").write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
print("done")
EOF

}'

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Anchor chunks to audio timestamps
command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat > align/anchor.js <<'EOF'
const fs = require("fs");
const norm = s => s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]+/g, " ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
const stimuli = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("public/content/stimuli.json", "utf8"));

/* Best in-order match of `needle` tokens starting at or after `from`.
   Greedy forward match allowing skips; window capped at 2.5x needle length. */
function bestMatch(hay, needle, from) {
  let best = { score: 0, start: -1, end: -1 };
  const cap = Math.ceil(needle.length * 2.5) + 6;
  for (let i = from; i < hay.length; i++) {
    if (hay[i] !== needle[0]) continue;
    let ni = 0, last = i;
    for (let j = i; j < Math.min(hay.length, i + cap) && ni < needle.length; j++) {
      if (hay[j] === needle[ni]) { ni++; last = j; }
    }
    const score = ni / needle.length;
    if (score > best.score) best = { score, start: i, end: last };
    if (best.score === 1) break;
  }
  return best;
}

const report = {};
for (const slug of Object.keys(stimuli)) {
  const words = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`align/${slug}.words.json`, "utf8"));
  const toks = words.map(w => norm(w.w)).map(w => w || "~");
  const chunks = stimuli[slug].chunks;
  let cursor = 0;
  const anchors = [];
  for (const c of chunks) {
    const needle = norm(c.text).split(" ");
    const m = bestMatch(toks, needle, cursor);
    if (m.start >= 0) {
      anchors.push({ n: c.n, score: +m.score.toFixed(3), startIdx: m.start, endIdx: m.end,
                     tStart: words[m.start].start, tEnd: words[m.end].end });
      cursor = m.end + 1;
    } else anchors.push({ n: c.n, score: 0 });
  }
  // gaps, to find the Space press before The Recall
  const gaps = [];
  for (let i = 1; i < words.length; i++) {
    const g = words[i].start - words[i - 1].end;
    if (g > 0.7) gaps.push({ afterIdx: i - 1, t: +words[i - 1].end.toFixed(2), gap: +g.toFixed(2) });
  }

  console.log(`\n=== ${slug} (${words.length} words, ends ${words[words.length-1].end.toFixed(1)}s)`);

  console.log("  biggest gaps:", report[slug].topGaps.map(g => `${g.t}s(+${g.gap})`).join(" "));
}
fs.writeFileSync("align/anchors.json", JSON.stringify(report, null, 1));
EOF

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

## The apology / accountability move

One of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When...

# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

### "Recall Rephrase Colour" → "Read, Recall, Colour"
The door's heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line
under a chip reading "Read, Recall, Colour". "Rephrase" belongs to a different
Activity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why
2,600 passing tests never caught it.
**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that
it matches.

### "Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…" → plain instruction
Clinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read "with full
intonation" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation
internal, "hear it naturally in your head", had been dropped in an earlier edit.
**Lesson:** a word like "intake" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,
check what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.

### "Three beats through the stimulus" → "Three steps"
"Beat" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary
says students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four
things Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.
**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.

### "22 pieces to read" → line removed
A **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same
page use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so "22 pieces" read as 22
sentences.
**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word
already means on the page before reusing it.

### "say it back" → "tell it back", "The Read" → "The Recall"
The library door still carried both retired names. "The Read" was step four's
former name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.
**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was
[/external_agent_tool_result]

[external_agent_tool_result]
### Read, Recall, Colour

The four-step vocabulary of the RRC runner, plus the language its grading uses.
The source method's own words for these steps are listed under `_Avoid_` and are
never used in the product.

**Chunk**:
An authored cut of a stimulus, roughly a sentence, carrying the Details that fall inside it. A guide for her eye and the unit content is written in, never a boundary she is held to: she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes.
_Avoid_: Line, segment, piece, part

**Read** (step 1):
The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken.
_Avoid_: Skim, study

**Retelling** (step 2):
Saying a chunk back out loud in her own words. **Retelling** is the word we use to each other and in code; the label a student reads is always **Tell it back**.
_Avoid_: Reading Recall (its former name, which put "Recall" on two different steps), Say it back (the old student label), translate, translation, paraphrase, summary, repeat-back

**Colouring In** (step 3):
Her spoken reaction to the chunk she just recalled — a thought, doubt, example, or joke that fits that chunk and no other.
_Avoid_: Quip, comment, annotation, reaction

**The Recall** (step 4):
The end of a stimulus, worked with the text gone: she says the whole argument back from memory. Graded on Coverage.
_Avoid_: Retelling (that is step 2), CLIR, recap, rebound, takeaway, The Read (see below: that is now the other number, not this step's former name)

**The Read**:
The analytical takeaway, and the second graded number: what the argument is DOING, as opposed to what it says. Authored per stimulus as `the_read`, judged as two flags, `read_given` and `read_matches` ([ADR 0025](../../../docs/adr/0025-the-read-is-the-second-number.md)). It is a dimension of the grading, never a fifth step she is asked to perform out loud.

It was step 4's name until [ADR 0012](../../../docs/adr/0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md) renamed that step to The Recall, and this entry did not exist for the six days between 0012 and 0025, so "The Read" read as simply retired. It is not. On the Report Card, **Your Read** is correct and must not be collapsed into The Recall: they are the reasoning half and the memory half, and they fail differently.
_Avoid_: Takeaway, the point, the gist, analysis

**the move**:
What a stimulus's Read turns on, in the judge's and the card's shorthand: the loophole, inference or conflict the argument runs on. Always defined on a surface before it is used, because "you found the move" means nothing to a student reading it for the first time.
_Avoid_: The trick, the gotcha, the key (reserved for the authored answer)

**Detail**:
One of the small authored facts a stimulus contains. PT102.S3.Q1 holds 21.
_Avoid_: Point, fact, bullet, item (reserved for an Activity's prompt)

**Coverage**:
How many of a stimulus's Details she carried, counted across everything she said about it rather than against any one Chunk.
_Avoid_: Accuracy, completeness, recall rate
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### Q9 - Resolution of the missing Recall
- Asked: how to handle the missing recall phase?
- Captured (verbatim): "make one wih t hte vice nd make sureti follows what we

- Read as: **generate the missing Recall audio in his voice** (clone from his own

  hold it to the criteria already agreed.
- Decision I am taking unasked, per his own "say what's actually true" rule: the
  generated Recall carries a **visible on-screen label** marking it a
  demonstration rather than his recording. Students must not believe it is the
  standard he recorded when it is not.

### Vocabulary corrected against the real glossary
`src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md` defines the four steps. My earlier
description was imprecise. Correctly:
1. **Read** - silent reading of a Chunk. 2. **Retelling**, student label
**"Tell it back"**. 3. **Colouring In** - the spoken reaction. 4. **The Recall** -
whole argument from memory, text gone, graded on Coverage.
Separately, **The Read** is NOT a step: it is the analytical takeaway and the
second graded number (`read_given`, `read_matches`, ADR 0025).

**Therefore the precise finding:** his recordings contain steps 1, 2, 3 AND The
Read (the closing analytical takeaway). Only **step 4, The Recall**, is absent.

Banned words for on-screen copy: Line, segment, piece, part (say **Chunk**);
"Say it back" (say **Tell it back**); takeaway/gist/analysis (say **The Read**);
point/fact/bullet (say **Detail**); no em dashes, ever.
EOF
echo saved

{
  "PT105_S2_Q1": "Okay. So some French divers recently found this big cave along the coast of the Mediterranean Sea. And the only way into it is through an underwater tunnel. The inside of the cave is completely filled with seawater, and it's got a lot of these big stalagmites in it, which are stony pillars that form when drops of water fall on the same single spot on the cave floor over and over, and they leave behind mineral deposits that build up over time.",
  "PT105_S2_Q2": "Okay. And to support that, the director says vaccine sales are likely to be lower, because each vaccine only gets administered to a patient once, whereas medicines that treat diseases and chronic illnesses get administered many times to each patient.",
  "PT105_S2_Q3": "Okay. So the manager is saying they've got this new computer network going in, and the purpose of it is to increase productivity. And they can install it during the day, which would disrupt the employees' work, or they can install it at night, which would mean much higher installation charges. And since saving money is important, the manager concludes they should have the network installed during the day."
}
EOF
node -e '
const fs=require("fs");
const s=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("recall/scripts.json","utf8"));
const st=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("public/content/stimuli.json","utf8"));
const norm=x=>x.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]+/g," ").replace(/\s+/g," ").trim();
const stop=new Set("the a an of to and is are was were that this it in on for be been by as with from at or its their they them he she his her only once each any other".split(" "));
let bad=0;
for(const slug of Object.keys(s)){
  const said=new Set(norm(s[slug]).split(" "));
  const details=st[slug].chunks.flatMap(c=>c.details);
  const missing=[];
  for(const d of details){
    const key=norm(d.text).split(" ").filter(w=>!stop.has(w)&&w.length>2);
    const hit=key.filter(w=>said.has(w)||[...said].some(x=>x.startsWith(w.slice(0,5))));
    if(hit.length/Math.max(key.length,1) < 0.5) missing.push(d.text);
  }
  bad+=missing.length;
  console.log(`${slug}: ${details.length} Details, ${details.length-missing.length} carried, ${missing.length} weak`);
  missing.forEach(m=>console.log("    WEAK:",m));
  console.log("    words:",norm(s[slug]).split(" ").length,"| est",(norm(s[slug]).split(" ").length/2.6).toFixed(0)+"s");
}
console.log(bad===0?"\nALL DETAILS CARRIED":"\n"+bad+" details need review");
'
[/external_agent_tool_call]

node -e '
const fs=require("fs");
const s=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("recall/scripts.json","utf8"));
const st=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("public/content/stimuli.json","utf8"));
const norm=x=>x.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]+/g," ").replace(/\s+/g," ").trim();
const stop=new Set("the a an of to and is are was were that this it in on for be been by as with from at or its their they them he she his her only once each any other".split(" "));
const stem=w=>w.replace(/(ing|ed|s|y)$/,"");
for(const slug of Object.keys(s)){
  const said=new Set(norm(s[slug]).split(" ").map(stem));
  const details=st[slug].chunks.flatMap(c=>c.details);
  const weak=details.filter(d=>{
    const key=norm(d.text).split(" ").filter(w=>!stop.has(w)&&w.length>2).map(stem);

description: Build step-accurate timeline from walkthroughs
command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat > align/timeline.js <<'EOF'
const fs = require("fs");
const norm = s => s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]+/g, " ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
const wt = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("align/walkthroughs.json", "utf8"));
const stimuli = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("public/content/stimuli.json", "utf8"));

/* Walkthrough shape: blockquote lines are the chunks he READ, in groups;
   the paragraph after a group is his Tell-it-back + Colouring In for that group. */
function groups(md) {
  const out = []; let cur = null;
  for (const raw of md.split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const line = raw.trim();
    if (!line) continue;
    if (line.startsWith(">")) {
      const t = line.replace(/^>\s*/, "");
      if (!cur || cur.said) { cur = { read: [], said: "" }; out.push(cur); }
      cur.read.push(t);
    } else if (cur) cur.said += (cur.said ? " " : "") + line;
  }
  return out.filter(g => g.read.length);
}

/* Find `needle` tokens in order starting at/after `from`. Returns span + score. */
function find(hay, needle, from) {
  let best = { score: 0, s: -1, e: -1 };
  const cap = Math.ceil(needle.length * 2.2) + 8;
  for (let i = from; i < hay.length; i++) {
    if (hay[i].slice(0, 4) !== needle[0].slice(0, 4)) continue;
    let ni = 0, last = i;
    for (let j = i; j < Math.min(hay.length, i + cap) && ni < needle.length; j++)
      if (hay[j].slice(0, 4) === needle[ni].slice(0, 4)) { ni++; last = j; }
    const sc = ni / needle.length;
    if (sc > best.score) best = { score: sc, s: i, e: last };
    if (sc > 0.95) break;
  }
  return best;
}

const timeline = {};
for (const slug of Object.keys(stimuli)) {
  const words = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`align/${slug}.words.json`, "utf8"));
  const toks = words.map(w => norm(w.w) || "~");
  const gs = groups(wt[slug]);
  const chunkTexts = stimuli[slug].chunks.map(c => norm(c.text));
  const segs = []; let cursor = 0;
  gs.forEach((g, gi) => {
    const needle = norm(g.read.join(" ")).split(" ");
    const m = find(toks, needle, cursor);
    if (m.s < 0 || m.score < 0.6) { segs.push({ gi, ok: false, score: m.score }); return; }
    // which authored chunk numbers this group covers
    const nums = g.read.map(r => {
      const rn = norm(r);
      let bi = -1, bs = 0;
      chunkTexts.forEach((ct, i) => { const a = new Set(ct.split(" ")); const b = rn.split(" ");
        const s = b.filter(x => a.has(x)).length / Math.max(b.length, 1); if (s > bs) { bs = s; bi = i; } });
      return bi >= 0 ? stimuli[slug].chunks[bi].n : null;
    }).filter(Boolean);
    segs.push({ gi, ok: true, score: +m.score.toFixed(3), chunkNs: [...new Set(nums)],
                readStart: words[m.s].start, readEnd: words[m.e].end, sIdx: m.s, eIdx: m.e });
    cursor = m.e + 1;
  });
  // Tell-it-back + Colouring In runs from end of a read to the next read's start
  const ok = segs.filter(s => s.ok);
  ok.forEach((s, i) => { s.workEnd = i + 1 < ok.length ? ok[i + 1].readStart : words[words.length - 1].end; });
  timeline[slug] = { duration: words[words.length - 1].end, segments: ok,
                     failed: segs.filter(s => !s.ok).length };
  console.log(`\n${slug}: ${ok.length} groups, ${timeline[slug].failed} failed`);
  ok.forEach(s => console.log(`   chunks[${s.chunkNs}] READ ${s.readStart.toFixed(1)}-${s.readEnd.toFixed(1)}s (${(s.score*100).toFixed(0)}%) then TELL+COLOUR to ${s.workEnd.toFixed(1)}s`));
}
fs.writeFileSync("public/content/timeline.json", JSON.stringify(timeline, null, 1));
EOF

description: Global alignment timeline
command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat > align/timeline.js <<'EOF'
const fs = require("fs");
const norm = s => s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9 ]+/g, " ").replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
const wt = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("align/walkthroughs.json", "utf8"));
const stimuli = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync("public/content/stimuli.json", "utf8"));

function groups(md) {
  const out = []; let cur = null;
  for (const raw of md.split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const line = raw.trim(); if (!line) continue;
    if (line.startsWith(">")) {
      const t = line.replace(/^>\s*/, "");
      if (!cur || cur.said) { cur = { read: [], said: "" }; out.push(cur); }
      cur.read.push(t);
    } else if (cur) cur.said += (cur.said ? " " : "") + line;
  }
  return out.filter(g => g.read.length);
}

/* Global Needleman-Wunsch over token sequences.
   Returns map[i] = index in B for each token i of A, or -1 when unaligned. */
function align(A, B) {
  const n = A.length, m = B.length;
  const M = 2, MIS = -1, GAP = -1;
  const eq = (a, b) => a === b || (a.length > 3 && b.length > 3 && a.slice(0, 4) === b.slice(0, 4));
  // score matrix as Int32Array rows
  const dp = new Int32Array((n + 1) * (m + 1));
  const at = (i, j) => i * (m + 1) + j;
  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) dp[at(i, 0)] = i * GAP;
  for (let j = 1; j <= m; j++) dp[at(0, j)] = j * GAP;
  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++)
    for (let j = 1; j <= m; j++) {
      const d = dp[at(i - 1, j - 1)] + (eq(A[i - 1], B[j - 1]) ? M : MIS);
      const u = dp[at(i - 1, j)] + GAP, l = dp[at(i, j - 1)] + GAP;
      dp[at(i, j)] = d >= u ? (d >= l ? d : l) : (u >= l ? u : l);
    }
  const map = new Array(n).fill(-1);
  let i = n, j = m;
  while (i > 0 && j > 0) {
    const d = dp[at(i - 1, j - 1)] + (eq(A[i - 1], B[j - 1]) ? M : MIS);
    if (dp[at(i, j)] === d) { if (eq(A[i - 1], B[j - 1])) map[i - 1] = j - 1; i--; j--; }
    else if (dp[at(i, j)] === dp[at(i - 1, j)] + GAP) i--;
    else j--;
  }
  return map;
}

const timeline = {};
for (const slug of Object.keys(stimuli)) {
  const words = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`align/${slug}.words.json`, "utf8"));
  const spoken = words.map(w => norm(w.w) || "~");
  const gs = groups(wt[slug]);

  // Flatten the walkthrough into tokens, remembering which group/part each came from
  const A = [], owner = [];
  gs.forEach((g, gi) => {
    norm(g.read.join(" ")).split(" ").forEach(t => { A.push(t); owner.push({ gi, part: "read" }); });
    norm(g.said).split(" ").filter(Boolean).forEach(t => { A.push(t); owner.push({ gi, part: "said" }); });
  });

  const map = align(A, spoken);
  const aligned = map.filter(x => x >= 0).length;

  const segs = gs.map((g, gi) => {
    const idx = p => { const o = []; for (let k = 0; k < A.length; k++)
      if (owner[k].gi === gi && owner[k].part === p && map[k] >= 0) o.push(map[k]); return o; };
    const r = idx("read"), s = idx("said");
    const chunkTexts = stimuli[slug].chunks.map(c => norm(c.text));
    const nums = g.read.map(t => { const b = norm(t).split(" "); let bi = -1, bs = 0;
      chunkTexts.forEach((ct, i) => { const set = new Set(ct.split(" "));
        const sc = b.filter(x => set.has(x)).length / Math.max(b.length, 1);
        if (sc > bs) { bs = sc; bi = i; } });
      return bi >= 0 ? stimuli[slug].chunks[bi].n : null; }).filter(Boolean);
    if (!r.length) return null;
    return { gi, chunkNs: [...new Set(nums)],
      readStart: words[Math.min(...r)].start, readEnd: words[Math.max(...r)].end,
      saidStart: s.length ? words[Math.min(...s)].start : null,
      workEnd: s.length ? words[Math.max(...s)].end : words[Math.max(...r)].end,
      readCoverage: +(r.length / norm(g.read.join(" ")).split(" ").length).toFixed(2) };
  }).filter(Boolean);
  // close gaps: each group runs until the next group's read begins
  segs.forEach((s, i) => { if (i + 1 < segs.length) s.workEnd = segs[i + 1].readStart; });

  timeline[slug] = { duration: words[words.length - 1].end, alignedPct: +(100 * aligned / A.length).toFixed(1), segments: segs };
  console.log(`\n${slug}: walkthrough ${A.length} tok, ${timeline[slug].alignedPct}% aligned to audio, ${segs.length} groups`);
  segs.forEach(s => console.log(`   chunks[${s.chunkNs}] READ ${s.readStart.toFixed(1)}-${s.readEnd.toFixed(1)}s (cov ${s.readCoverage}) TELL+COLOUR -> ${s.workEnd.toFixed(1)}s`));
}
fs.writeFileSync("public/content/timeline.json", JSON.stringify(timeline, null, 1));
EOF

command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && node -e '
const fs=require("fs");
// caption cues: group whisper words into short readable lines with timings
for(const slug of ["PT105_S2_Q1","PT105_S2_Q2","PT105_S2_Q3"]){
  const w=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`align/${slug}.words.json`,"utf8"));
  const cues=[]; let cur=null;
  for(let i=0;i<w.length;i++){
    if(!cur) cur={start:w[i].start,end:w[i].end,text:w[i].w};
    else {cur.text+=" "+w[i].w; cur.end=w[i].end;}
    const gap=i+1<w.length? w[i+1].start-w[i].end : 9;
    if(cur.text.length>=62 || gap>0.55 || /[.?!]$/.test(w[i].w)&&cur.text.length>34){cues.push(cur);cur=null;}
  }
  if(cur)cues.push(cur);
  fs.writeFileSync(`public/content/${slug}.cues.json`,JSON.stringify(cues));
  console.log(slug,cues.length,"caption cues, longest",Math.max(...cues.map(c=>c.text.length)),"chars");
}'

import React from "react";

/* A faithful replica of `SectionStage`: indigo field, white header rail, one
   centred white paper card. Presentational only, exactly as the real one is. */
export const Screen: React.FC<{
  children: React.ReactNode;
  through: number;      // progress 0..1
  clock: string;
  where: string;
  instruction: string;
  keyDoes: string;
}> = ({ children, through, clock, where, instruction, keyDoes }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H, transform: `scale(${SCALE})`,
      transformOrigin: "top left", background: SEC.field,
      fontFamily: SEC.sans, display: "flex", flexDirection: "column",
    }}
  >
    {/* header rail */}
    <header
      style={{
        display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 16,
        padding: "0.75rem 1.5rem", borderBottom: `1px solid ${SEC.hairline}`,
        background: SEC.paper, color: SEC.chrome, fontSize: 13,
      }}
    >
      <span style={{ fontWeight: 500 }}>{where}</span>
      <div style={{ flex: 1, height: 3, background: SEC.hairline, borderRadius: 2, overflow: "hidden" }}>
        <div style={{ height: "100%", width: "100%", background: SEC.chrome,
                      transform: `scaleX(${through})`, transformOrigin: "left" }} />
      </div>
      <span style={{ fontVariantNumeric: "tabular-nums" }}>{clock}</span>
      <span style={{ display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 6, color: "#b91c1c" }}>
        <i style={{ width: 8, height: 8, borderRadius: 99, background: "#b91c1c", display: "block" }} />
        REC
      </span>
    </header>

    {/* field + paper */}
    <main style={{ flex: 1, display: "flex", padding: "1rem", minHeight: 0 }}>
      <div
        style={{
          display: "flex", flexDirection: "column", width: "100%", maxWidth: 1400,
          margin: "0 auto", border: `1px solid ${SEC.rule}`, borderRadius: 6,
          background: SEC.paper, overflow: "hidden",
          boxShadow: "0 4px 18px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.35)",
        }}
      >
        <div style={{ flex: 1, display: "flex", justifyContent: "center", padding: "2rem 1.5rem", minHeight: 0 }}>

);

);

import React from "react";
import { SEC, TEACH } from "./tokens";

export type Chunk = { n: number; text: string; details: { n: number; text: string }[] };

/* The stimulus exactly as the drill renders it: every chunk in order, nothing
   marking one out from another. The teaching highlight is layered ON TOP and is
   the only thing here the real screen does not do. */
export const Stimulus: React.FC<{
  chunks: Chunk[];
  activeNs: number[];
  reading: boolean;
}> = ({ chunks, activeNs, reading }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      maxWidth: "52ch", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,
      fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,
      fontOpticalSizing: "none", fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
    }}
  >
    {chunks.map((c) => {
      const on = activeNs.includes(c.n);
      return (
        <span
          key={c.n}
          style={{
            background: on ? TEACH.amberSoft : "transparent",
            boxShadow: on ? `inset 0 -2px 0 ${TEACH.amber}` : "none",
            borderRadius: 2,
            transition: "background 200ms",
          }}
        >
          {c.text}{" "}
        </span>
      );
    })}
  </div>
);

export const Captions: React.FC<{ text: string }> = ({ text }) =>
  text ? (
    <div style={{
      position: "absolute", bottom: 118, left: "50%", transform: "translateX(-50%)",
      maxWidth: 1180, padding: "14px 26px", borderRadius: 10,
      background: "rgba(8,6,20,0.9)", color: "#f8fafc",
      fontFamily: SEC.sans, fontSize: 30, lineHeight: 1.34, textAlign: "center",
    }}>
      {text}
    </div>
  ) : null;

/* Names the step she is in. The label a student reads for step 2 is always
   "Tell it back" (CONTEXT.md glossary), never "Say it back". */
export const StepRail: React.FC<{ step: string; note: string }> = ({ step, note }) => (
  <div style={{
    position: "absolute", left: 32, top: 28, zIndex: 5, width: 300,
    padding: "18px 22px", borderRadius: 12,
    border: `1.5px solid ${TEACH.amber}`, background: TEACH.panel,
    fontFamily: SEC.sans,
  }}>
    <div style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 15, letterSpacing: 1.4, textTransform: "uppercase" }}>
      What he is doing
    </div>
    <div style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 32, marginTop: 8, fontWeight: 500 }}>{step}</div>
    <div style={{ color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 19, marginTop: 10, lineHeight: 1.45 }}>{note}</div>
  </div>
);
