import React from "react";
import { AbsoluteFill, Audio, staticFile, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig, interpolate } from "remotion";
import { Screen, TeachBadge } from "./Screen";
import { Stimulus, Captions, StepRail, type Chunk } from "./Stimulus";
import { SEC, TEACH } from "./tokens";

const mmss = (s: number) => `${Math.floor(s / 60)}:${String(Math.floor(s % 60)).padStart(2, "0")}`;

export type Seg = {
  chunkNs: number[]; readStart: number; readEnd: number;
  saidStart: number | null; workEnd: number;
};
export type Cue = { start: number; end: number; text: string };

/* One stimulus, worked. `t` is seconds into THIS recording, so the audio and the
   overlay are driven by the same clock and cannot drift. */
export const Working: React.FC<{
  slug: string; chunks: Chunk[]; segments: Seg[]; cues: Cue[];
  where: string; qIndex: number; qTotal: number;
}> = ({ slug, chunks, segments, cues, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const { fps, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();
  const t = frame / fps;

  const seg = segments.find((s) => t >= s.readStart && t < s.workEnd)
    ?? (t < segments[0]?.readStart ? undefined : segments[segments.length - 1]);
  const reading = !!seg && t >= seg.readStart && t < seg.readEnd;
  const activeNs = seg ? seg.chunkNs : [];

  const step = !seg ? "About to start"
    : reading ? "Read"
    : "Tell it back, then colour it in";
  const note = !seg ? "The whole stimulus is already on screen."
    : reading ? "Reading the chunk. Nothing is said out loud yet."
    : "Saying that chunk back in his own words, then reacting to it.";

  const cue = cues.find((c) => t >= c.start && t <= c.end);

  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field }}>
      <Audio src={staticFile(`audio/${slug}.mp3`)} />
      <Screen
        through={frame / durationInFrames}
        clock={mmss(t)}
        where={`${where} · ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}
        instruction="Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go."
        keyDoes="recall full stimulus"
      >
        <Stimulus chunks={chunks} activeNs={activeNs} reading={reading} />
      </Screen>
      <StepRail step={step} note={note} />
      <TeachBadge>Teaching overlay, not part of the drill</TeachBadge>
      <Captions text={cue?.text ?? ""} />
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

/* Step 4. Taught rather than demonstrated: the recordings contain steps 1 to 3
   and The Read, and no Recall exists to play. Verified 2026-09-09 against the
   Whisper transcripts, the stored Deepgram text and walkthrough_md. */
export const RecallCard: React.FC<{ detailCount: number }> = ({ detailCount }) => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
  const o = interpolate(frame, [0, 12], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" });
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field, opacity: o,
      justifyContent: "center", alignItems: "center", fontFamily: SEC.sans, padding: 120 }}>
      <div style={{ maxWidth: 1180 }}>
        <div style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 22, letterSpacing: 2, textTransform: "uppercase" }}>
          Step 4
        </div>
        <div style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 82, fontWeight: 500, marginTop: 14 }}>The Recall</div>
        <div style={{ color: "#cbd5e1", fontSize: 34, lineHeight: 1.5, marginTop: 30 }}>
          Press Space a second time. The stimulus disappears and you say the whole
          argument back from memory.
        </div>
        <div style={{ color: "#cbd5e1", fontSize: 34, lineHeight: 1.5, marginTop: 22 }}>
          This is what Coverage is scored on. On this stimulus there are{" "}
          <span style={{ color: TEACH.amber }}>{detailCount} Details</span> to carry.
        </div>
      </div>
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

export const TitleCard: React.FC = () => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const o = interpolate(frame, [0, 14], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" });
  const rules = [
    "One take, all the way through, out loud.",
    "Space twice per stimulus: once when you have read it all, once when you have given The Recall.",
    "Nothing is judged until the whole section is done.",
  ];
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field, opacity: o, justifyContent: "center",
      padding: "0 150px", fontFamily: SEC.sans }}>
      <div style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 22, letterSpacing: 2.4, textTransform: "uppercase" }}>
        Read, Recall, Colour
      </div>
      <div style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 76, fontWeight: 500, marginTop: 16, lineHeight: 1.12 }}>
        What a run actually looks like
      </div>
      <div style={{ marginTop: 46 }}>
        {rules.map((r, i) => {
          const ro = interpolate(frame, [22 + i * 16, 40 + i * 16], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp", extrapolateLeft: "clamp" });
          return (
            <div key={i} style={{ opacity: ro, display: "flex", gap: 20, marginBottom: 24 }}>
              <span style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 32 }}>{i + 1}</span>
              <span style={{ color: "#e2e8f0", fontSize: 32, lineHeight: 1.45, maxWidth: 1240 }}>{r}</span>
            </div>
          );
        })}
      </div>
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

export const ReadyBeat: React.FC = () => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const { fps, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();
  const left = Math.max(0, Math.ceil((durationInFrames - frame) / fps));
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field }}>
      <Screen through={0} clock="0:00" where="PT105 Section 2 · 1 of 23"
        instruction="Say something to check your microphone." keyDoes="recall full stimulus">
        <div style={{ textAlign: "center", fontFamily: SEC.sans, alignSelf: "center" }}>
          <div style={{ color: SEC.ink, fontSize: 24, fontWeight: 500 }}>Recording now</div>
          <div style={{ color: SEC.chrome, fontSize: 15, marginTop: 12, maxWidth: 430, lineHeight: 1.5 }}>
            One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus: once
            when you have read it all, once when you have given The Recall.
          </div>
          <div style={{ color: SEC.chrome, fontSize: 15, marginTop: 18 }}>
            {left > 0 ? `Starting in ${left}` : "Starting"}
          </div>
        </div>
      </Screen>
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

export const Outro: React.FC = () => {
  const frame = useCurrentFrame();
  const o = interpolate(frame, [0, 14], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" });
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field, opacity: o, justifyContent: "center",
      padding: "0 150px", fontFamily: SEC.sans }}>
      <div style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 64, fontWeight: 500, lineHeight: 1.15 }}>
        Then you get a Report Card.
      </div>
      <div style={{ color: "#cbd5e1", fontSize: 34, marginTop: 28, lineHeight: 1.5, maxWidth: 1280 }}>
        Every stimulus marked for what you carried and what you dropped, your own
        recording to hear back, and the same stimulus done properly.
      </div>
      <div style={{ marginTop: 40, display: "inline-flex", gap: 16 }}>
        {["Hear it back", "Hear it done right"].map((b) => (
          <span key={b} style={{ padding: "16px 30px", borderRadius: 10,
            border: `1px solid ${TEACH.amber}`, color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 27 }}>{b}</span>
        ))}
      </div>
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

description: Write root composition and bundle
command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat > src/rrc/RRC.tsx <<'TSX'
import React from "react";
import { AbsoluteFill, Sequence, useVideoConfig } from "remotion";
import { loadFont as loadFraunces } from "@remotion/google-fonts/Fraunces";
import { TitleCard, ReadyBeat, Working, RecallCard, Outro, type Seg, type Cue } from "./Scenes";
import type { Chunk } from "./Stimulus";
import { SEC } from "./tokens";

loadFraunces();

export type Payload = {
  stimuli: Record<string, { slug: string; chunks: Chunk[] }>;
  timeline: Record<string, { duration: number; segments: Seg[] }>;
  cues: Record<string, Cue[]>;
};

export const ORDER = ["PT105_S2_Q1", "PT105_S2_Q2", "PT105_S2_Q3"];
export const TITLE_S = 9, READY_S = 4, RECALL_S = 9, OUTRO_S = 9;

/** Frame-exact plan, so duration is derived once and never guessed twice. */
export function plan(timeline: Payload["timeline"], fps: number) {
  const out: { name: string; from: number; frames: number; slug?: string }[] = [];
  let f = 0;
  const push = (name: string, secs: number, slug?: string) => {
    const frames = Math.round(secs * fps);
    out.push({ name, from: f, frames, slug }); f += frames;
  };
  push("title", TITLE_S);
  push("ready", READY_S);
  for (const slug of ORDER) {
    push(`work:${slug}`, timeline[slug].duration, slug);
    push(`recall:${slug}`, RECALL_S, slug);
  }
  push("outro", OUTRO_S);
  return { scenes: out, total: f };
}

export const RRC: React.FC<Payload> = ({ stimuli, timeline, cues }) => {
  const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
  const { scenes } = plan(timeline, fps);
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field }}>
      {scenes.map((s) => {
        const i = s.slug ? ORDER.indexOf(s.slug) : -1;
        let inner: React.ReactNode = null;
        if (s.name === "title") inner = <TitleCard />;
        else if (s.name === "ready") inner = <ReadyBeat />;
        else if (s.name.startsWith("work:") && s.slug)
          inner = (
            <Working
              slug={s.slug}
              chunks={stimuli[s.slug].chunks}
              segments={timeline[s.slug].segments}
              cues={cues[s.slug]}
              where="PT105 Section 2"
              qIndex={i + 1}
              qTotal={23}
            />
          );
        else if (s.name.startsWith("recall:") && s.slug)
          inner = <RecallCard detailCount={stimuli[s.slug].chunks.reduce((a, c) => a + c.details.length, 0)} />;
        else if (s.name === "outro") inner = <Outro />;
        return (
          <Sequence key={s.name} from={s.from} durationInFrames={s.frames} name={s.name}>
            {inner}
          </Sequence>
        );
      })}
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

import { Composition, staticFile } from "remotion";
import { RRC, plan, ORDER, type Payload } from "./rrc/RRC";

import stimuli from "../public/content/stimuli.json";
import timeline from "../public/content/timeline.json";

};

export const RemotionRoot: React.FC = () => (

    id="RRCWalkthrough"
    component={RRC as never}
    durationInFrames={plan(payload.timeline, FPS).total}
    fps={FPS}
    width={1920}
    height={1080}
    defaultProps={payload as never}
  />
);

command: cd ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat > src/rrc/Stage.tsx <<'TSX'
import React from "react";
import { SEC, TEACH, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from "./tokens";

/* Layout law for this video: the teaching layer gets its own 96px band across
   the top and NEVER overlaps the replicated app. The first cut floated the rail
   and the badge over the app's header rail and clipped it, which is exactly the
   confusion the Q1 decision forbids. */
export const BAND_H = 96;
const APP_H = 1080 - BAND_H;
export const APP_SCALE = APP_H / LOGICAL_H;          // 984 / 720
const APP_W = LOGICAL_W * APP_SCALE;
export const APP_X = (1920 - APP_W) / 2;

export const TeachBand: React.FC<{ step: string; note: string }> = ({ step, note }) => (
  <div style={{
    position: "absolute", top: 0, left: 0, width: 1920, height: BAND_H, zIndex: 6,
    display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 20, padding: "0 34px",
    background: "#0d0a1f", borderBottom: `2px solid ${TEACH.amber}`,
    fontFamily: SEC.sans, boxSizing: "border-box",
  }}>
    <span style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 14, letterSpacing: 1.6,
                   textTransform: "uppercase", whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>
      What he is doing
    </span>
    <span style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 30, fontWeight: 500, whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>{step}</span>
    <span style={{ color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 20, opacity: 0.92 }}>{note}</span>
    <span style={{ marginLeft: "auto", display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 9,
                   padding: "8px 16px", borderRadius: 999, border: `1.5px solid ${TEACH.amber}`,
                   color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 16, whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>
      <span style={{ width: 8, height: 8, borderRadius: 99, background: TEACH.amber }} />
      Teaching overlay, not part of the drill
    </span>
  </div>
);

/** Positions the replicated app under the band, never behind it. */
export const AppFrame: React.FC<{ children: React.ReactNode }> = ({ children }) => (
  <div style={{
    position: "absolute", top: BAND_H, left: APP_X,
    width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H,
    transform: `scale(${APP_SCALE})`, transformOrigin: "top left",
  }}>
    {children}
  </div>
);

  const mine=st[slug], theirs=src.items.find(i=>i.slug===slug);
  // 1. chunk text identical to authored content
  const same=mine.chunks.length===theirs.chunks.length &&
    mine.chunks.every((c,i)=>c.text===theirs.chunks[i].text && c.n===theirs.chunks[i].n);
  P(same,`${slug}: chunk text + numbering identical to authored content (${mine.chunks.length} chunks)`);
  // 2. concatenated chunks reconstruct the prompt
  const join=mine.chunks.map(c=>c.text).join(" ").replace(/\s+/g," ").trim();
  const prompt=theirs.prompt.replace(/\s+/g," ").trim();
  P(join===prompt,`${slug}: chunks concatenate back to the exact prompt`);
  // 3. details preserved
  const dm=mine.chunks.reduce((a,c)=>a+c.details.length,0);
  const dt=theirs.chunks.reduce((a,c)=>a+(c.details||[]).length,0);
  P(dm===dt,`${slug}: all ${dt} Details preserved`);
  // 4. timeline monotonic and inside audio
  const segs=tl[slug].segments; let mono=true;
  for(let i=0;i<segs.length;i++){
    if(segs[i].readStart>segs[i].readEnd||segs[i].readEnd>segs[i].workEnd) mono=false;
    if(i&&segs[i].readStart<segs[i-1].workEnd-0.001) mono=false;
    if(segs[i].workEnd>tl[slug].duration+0.01) mono=false;}
  P(mono,`${slug}: timeline monotonic, non-overlapping, within audio duration`);
  // 5. every chunk covered by exactly one segment
  const covered=segs.flatMap(s=>s.chunkNs).sort((a,b)=>a-b);
  const expect=mine.chunks.map(c=>c.n);
  P(JSON.stringify(covered)===JSON.stringify(expect),`${slug}: every chunk covered exactly once [${covered}]`);
  // 6. captions within audio
  const cues=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(`public/content/${slug}.cues.json`,"utf8"));
  P(cues.every(c=>c.start>=0&&c.end<=tl[slug].duration+0.05)&&cues.length>0,`${slug}: ${cues.length} caption cues within audio bounds`);
}
console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);'

const fs=require("fs");

// only string literals that reach the screen
let copy=[];
for(const f of files){
  const s=fs.readFileSync(f,"utf8");
  for(const m of s.matchAll(/"([^"\\]{6,200})"|>\s*([A-Z][^<>{}]{8,200}?)\s*</g)){
    const t=(m[1]||m[2]||"").trim();
    if(/^[a-z-]+$/.test(t)) continue;                     // css values
    if(/[#(){};:]|rgba|solid|px|rem|uppercase|tabular|Helvetica|Fraunces|ui-serif/.test(t)) continue;
    if(t) copy.push({f,t});
  }
}
const banned=[
 [/\u2014/,"em dash"],[/\bsay it back\b/i,'"say it back" (use "Tell it back")'],
 [/\blines?\b(?! of)/i,'"line" (use Chunk)'],[/\bsegments?\b/i,'"segment"'],
 [/\bpieces?\b/i,'"piece"'],[/\bparts?\b/i,'"part"'],
 [/\btakeaway\b/i,'"takeaway"'],[/\bthe gist\b/i,'"the gist"'],
 [/\bbullet\b/i,'"bullet"'],[/\bpoints? (to|she) carr/i,'"point" (use Detail)'],
 [/\bgrade\b/i,'"grade" (use Report Card)'],[/\bbeat\b/i,'"beat" (internal name)'],
];

}

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Write project README
command: cat > ~/dev/ai-os/projects/video/README.md <<'EOF'
# LSAT U video

Remotion project for LSAT U instructional video. Verified rendering 2026-09-09.

## The RRC walkthrough

`RRCWalkthrough` shows a real run of PT105 Section 2, questions 1 to 3, with
Joshua's own model-run recordings as the audio and a teaching overlay on top.

```bash
npm run dev                                  # studio
npx remotion bundle                          # build the bundle first
npx remotion render build RRCWalkthrough out/rrc-walkthrough.mp4
```

Render from a **built bundle**, not the default temp path. The temp-directory
render hits an ENOENT on this machine; bundling to `build/` first sidesteps it.

## Two machine-specific gotchas

- **rspack emits no JavaScript here.** `Config.setRspack(true)` (which
  `create-video` writes when you accept Tailwind) prints "Bundling 100%", exits
  0, and produces only `index.html`. The resulting `ENOENT ... bundle.js` is a
  decoy: it is the source-map reader failing, not the real fault. It is
  commented out in `remotion.config.ts`. Do not re-enable without testing a render.
- **npm blocks install scripts**, so esbuild's binary never installs. Fix with
  `npm approve-scripts esbuild`.

## How the overlay stays in sync

1. `align/transcribe.py` runs faster-whisper over the three MP3s for word-level
   timestamps. Agreement with the stored Deepgram transcripts is 96.8 to 98.0%.
2. `align/timeline.js` aligns `walkthrough_md` to that word stream with global
   Needleman-Wunsch, giving the exact span where each Chunk is read aloud.
   Fuzzy anchoring was tried first and drifted; do not go back to it.
3. `align/copycheck.js` checks on-screen copy against the banned vocabulary in
   `activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`.

Colours and type in `src/rrc/tokens.ts` are sampled from the real
`recall/section.css`. Re-sample rather than eyeball if it changes.

**Layout law:** the teaching layer owns a 96px band across the top and never
overlaps the replicated app. The first cut floated it over the app's header rail
and clipped it, which is exactly the confusion the design forbids.

## Known gap: The Recall

The model runs contain steps 1 to 3 (Read, Tell it back, Colouring In) and The
Read. They contain **no Recall**: nowhere in any of the three does he say the
whole argument back from memory. Verified against the Whisper transcripts, the
stored Deepgram text, and `walkthrough_md`.

So step 4 is currently **taught by a card, not demonstrated**. Scripts to fill it
are written and verified in `recall/scripts.json`, and a voice reference is cut
at `recall/voice-ref.mp3`. Generating the audio needs TTS credit, which this
machine has none of. When audio exists, drop it in and swap `RecallCard` for a
`Working`-style scene.
EOF

# Handoff: RRC instructional video

Joshua here. I accidentally ran this in a different chat than I meant to. We are
picking it up in this one. Below is everything that session did, so you have the
full picture without re-deriving it.

Date: 2026-09-09. Source session: `ai-os-9f`.

---

## What the task is

Build an instructional video showing a full Read, Recall, Colour run of 3
questions, with the stimulus rendered exactly as students see it in the runner,
narrated by Joshua's stored model-run recordings, so students understand each
step before they sit the drill.

Full requirements interview is captured at
`brainstorms/2026-09-09-rrc-instructional-video.md`. **Read that first.** It has
every decision, its reasoning, and the flags.

---

## What exists and works

**Remotion project at `projects/video`.** Verified rendering. A 5:24 video was
produced at `out/rrc-walkthrough.mp4` (21.5 MB, 1920x1080, 30fps).

Composition `RRCWalkthrough`: rules card, the real ready beat, PT105.S2 Q1-Q3
with Joshua's real audio, an amber teaching band naming the current step, burned
in captions, and a Report Card outro.

**Setup already done, do not redo:**
- Remotion 4.0.523, Tailwind v4, agent skills in `skills-lock.json`
- `remotion@remotion` Claude Code plugin installed (user scope)
- faster-whisper in `.venv`, word-level alignment for all three MP3s
- The three model-run MP3s downloaded byte-exact to `public/audio/`

**Two machine traps already solved (see `projects/video/README.md`):**
1. `Config.setRspack(true)` emits ZERO JavaScript on this box while exiting 0.
   The resulting `ENOENT ... bundle.js` is a decoy from the source-map reader,
   not the real fault. It is commented out. Do not re-enable.
2. npm blocks install scripts, so esbuild's binary never installed.
   `npm approve-scripts esbuild` fixes it.
3. Render from a built bundle: `npx remotion bundle` then
   `npx remotion render build RRCWalkthrough out/x.mp4`. The default temp-path
   render hits an ENOENT here.

---

## Key findings that took work to establish

**1. The model runs contain steps 1 to 3 plus The Read. They contain NO Recall.**
Verified three independent ways: Whisper transcripts, the stored Deepgram
`transcript_text`, and `walkthrough_md`. Nowhere in any of the three does he say
the whole argument back from memory. Step 4 currently ships as a card that
teaches The Recall rather than demonstrating it.

**2. He groups chunks when he works them.** Recovered from the audio:
- Q1: chunk 1 alone, chunk 2 alone, chunks 3+4 together
- Q2: chunks 1+2 together, chunks 3+4 together
- Q3: chunks 1+2 together, chunk 3 alone
This is ADR 0013 (she chunks freely) visible in his own run. The overlay follows
his grouping, not the authored chunk boundaries.

**3. `walkthrough_md` is the authoritative segmentation.** Blockquoted verbatim
chunk reads, each followed by his rephrase paragraph. Aligning it to the Whisper
word stream with global Needleman-Wunsch gives exact step timings
(Q1 91.7%, Q2 97.1%, Q3 92.0% aligned). An earlier greedy fuzzy anchor drifted
badly. Do not go back to it.

**4. Vocabulary is strict.** From `activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`:
Read (step 1, silent) / Retelling, student label **"Tell it back"** (step 2) /
**Colouring In** (step 3) / **The Recall** (step 4, text gone, graded on
Coverage). **The Read** is NOT a step: it is the analytical takeaway and the
second graded number. Banned in student copy: line, segment, piece, part,
"say it back", takeaway, gist, bullet, beat. **No em dashes, ever.**

**5. Data integrity flag:** `PT105_S2_Q13` and `PT105_S2_Q14` have byte-identical
audio (1672845B) and identical word counts. Almost certainly a duplicate upload.
Not investigated. Separate task.

---

## Verification already passed (18/18)

Per stimulus: chunk text and numbering identical to authored content; chunks
concatenate back to the exact prompt; all Details preserved (24/19/11); timeline
monotonic, non-overlapping, inside audio; every chunk covered exactly once;
caption cues in bounds. Zero em dashes in `src/`. Rendered duration 324.245s vs
predicted 324.20s. Audio confirmed audible in all three questions
(-6.6, -3.1, -5.2 dB peak); cards confirmed silent.

---

## Where it stopped: round 2, unresolved

Joshua reviewed the first cut and asked for five changes:

1. **Step 1 is silent and internal.** v1 reads as "read out loud then react",
   which is not the drill. Reading happens in your head. Then you look away (text
   may stay on screen), rephrase in your own words, then react.
2. A **step indicator on the side** that fades.
3. **The Recall demonstrated, not just taught:** show a spacebar being pressed,
   the screen clearing, then the whole stimulus repeated back from memory out
   loud. He wants a script written and audio made to fit.
4. **Pauses**, with the instruction shown bold before each demonstration.
5. The react step is named **Colouring In**.

### Two things block round 2

**A. The central contradiction, NOT yet resolved.**
His recordings have him reading each chunk **aloud, verbatim**, before
rephrasing. That is what makes the alignment possible. But step 1 is silent. So
the source audio demonstrates the exact behaviour the video must not teach.
Playing his voice reading the text while a caption says "read this in your head"
contradicts itself. Options put to him (he has not answered):
mute the reading and compress to a silent beat; mute but keep real duration;
keep audio and label it a recording artefact; or duck it to a murmur.
**He dismissed the question set. Nothing was changed. Ask him again.**

**B. The Recall audio cannot be produced on this machine.**
Higgsfield is at **0 credits on a free plan**, and there is no ElevenLabs, Azure,
Google or OpenAI TTS key in any `.env`. Already staged and waiting:
- Voice reference cut and uploaded successfully: `recall/voice-ref.mp3`,
  21.9s of clean natural speech from Q1, media_id `9016f3b6-65e5-482e-8efe-6ce518888c68`
- Three Recall scripts written and verified in `recall/scripts.json`, carrying
  52 of 54 Details lexically (the other two are structural, carried by "whereas"
  and "since")
Unblock by topping up credits, adding a TTS key, or Joshua recording the three
himself (about 90 seconds; arguably the best answer for a teaching brand).

---

## Also worth knowing

Separate research this session produced
`wiki/VIDEO-GENERATION-OPTIONS-2026-09-09.md`: Remotion is free for teams of up
to 3, so it costs nothing beyond the Claude Code membership; the official
Remotion MCP is **deprecated** (skills/plugin is the current path); Sora 2's API
shuts down 2026-09-24. Firecrawl research for it cost 4 credits total.
EOF

Where it started
Picked up from the Round 6 handoff. Joshua gave a new revision list (stimulus out of the way while reciting, highlight tighter, seamless motion, bubbles off the stimulus, 4K, realistic, first-person head turn) and said "grill me". Four grill questions were answered, then he delegated the rest ("I give you the liberty to do this in the best way possible"). After watching the result he asked for the cards to face the viewer square on, then to put the three cuts on the RRC drill page in LSAT U as demos, live.
Decisions locked + what shipped

* Head turn is real, from a cylindrical panorama of the same room, not a wider flat photo — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\align\pano\` (`make_config.py`, `build_pano.py`, `depth_pano.py`, `render_views.py`, `fit_left.py`, `fit_views.py` which failed and is kept as evidence). Plate hFOV assumed 66; rot45-3 placed by three hand-read seam features; seam on the door mullion; left bookshelf strip from outpaint-2 for theta < -21.
* One camera model, two consumers — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\src\pov\headTurn.ts` is the truth; `align\export_camera.ts` (run with plain `node`) writes `align\pano\camera.json`; Python refuses to render if its monitor projection disagrees (measured 0.0000 px).
* Turn is 40 deg (bezel-and-margin sliver ~6% of frame at the left edge), half turn 8 deg for card beats, neck pivot 0.09 m for parallax, 4 shutter samples per moving frame. 105 frames at 3840x2160 in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\public\plates\r7\seq\`.
* Screen centred straight ahead (R7-Q3); FACING_HFOV derived from Round 6's accepted monitor size.
* Every overlay is a room object projected through the same camera (`src\pov\World.tsx`, `anchors.ts`), and after his first viewing all of them are billboards (square on to the eye) centred on the turned line of sight (theta 40-42).
* "Look Away" is his verbatim wording, chrome serif italic sign with layered extrusion (`src\pov\LookAway.tsx`; the chrome face and the extrusion are two stacked copies because Chrome paints text-shadow over clipped fills).
* Bubbles, placard, intro cards (`src\pov\Cards.tsx`), Recall column (`src\pov\RecallRoom.tsx`), marker highlighter (`src\pov\Stimulus7.tsx`), room effects: blink, exposure adaptation, peripheral blur, vignette, drift (`src\pov\HeadRoom.tsx`). Beat-to-head rule in `src\pov\headState.ts`: turn away entering a tell/colour/recall run (LEAD 0.55 s off a read), back so the eye lands as the next beat starts.
* Peripheral blur is allowed as an optical effect (his Q3 words), which partially supersedes Round 6's no-blur rule; masking blur on the page stays banned.
